          <p class="in">(1) a social worker purchases goods or services from the client and a reasonable and prudent social worker would determine that it is not practical or reasonable to obtain the goods or services from another provider; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) engaging in the business relationship will not be detrimental to the client or the professional relationship.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.220.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Business relationship with former client.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A social worker may purchase goods or services from a former client or otherwise engage in a business relationship with a former client after appropriate termination of the professional relationship unless a reasonable and prudent social worker would conclude after appropriate assessment that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the former client is emotionally dependent on the social worker and purchasing goods or services from the former client or otherwise engaging in a business relationship with the former client would be detrimental to the former client; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the social worker is emotionally dependent on the former client and purchasing goods or services from the former client or otherwise engaging in a business relationship with the former client would be detrimental to the former client.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.220.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Previous sexual, personal, or business relationship.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A social worker must not engage in a social worker/client relationship with an individual with whom the social worker had a previous sexual relationship.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A social worker must not engage in a social worker/client relationship with an individual with whom the social worker had a previous personal or business relationship if a reasonable and prudent social worker would conclude after appropriate assessment that the social worker/client relationship would create an unacceptable risk of client harm or that the social worker's objectivity or professional judgment may be impaired.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.220.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Giving alcohol or other drugs to client.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Unless authorized by law, a social worker must not offer medication or controlled substances to a client.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A social worker must not accept medication or controlled substances from a client, except that if authorized by law, a social worker may accept medication or controlled substances from a client for purposes of disposal or to monitor use.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A social worker must not offer alcoholic beverages to a client except when the offer is authorized or prescribed by a physician or is offered according to a client's care plan.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A social worker must not accept alcoholic beverages from a client.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.220.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Relationship with client's family or household member.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Subdivisions 1 to 14 apply to a social worker's relationship with a client's family or household member when a reasonable and prudent social worker would conclude after appropriate assessment that a relationship with a family or household member would create an unacceptable risk of harm to the client.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.220.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.107.0">2007 c 123 s 107,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.225">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.225 TREATMENT AND INTERVENTION SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.225.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Assessment or diagnosis.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A social worker must base treatment and intervention services on an assessment or diagnosis. A social worker must evaluate, on an ongoing basis, the appropriateness of the assessment or diagnosis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.225.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Assessment or diagnostic instruments.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A social worker must not use an assessment or diagnostic instrument without adequate training. A social worker must follow standards and accepted procedures for using an assessment or diagnostic instrument. A social worker must inform a client of the purpose before administering the instrument and must make the results available to the client.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.225.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Plan for services.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A social worker must develop a plan for services that includes goals based on the assessment or diagnosis. A social worker must evaluate, on an ongoing basis, the appropriateness of the plan and the client's progress toward the goals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.225.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Records.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A social worker must make and maintain current and accurate records, appropriate to the circumstances, of all services provided to a client. At a minimum, the records must contain documentation of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the assessment or diagnosis;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the content of the service plan;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) progress with the plan and any revisions of assessment, diagnosis, or plan;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) any fees charged and payments made;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) copies of all client-written authorizations for release of information; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) other information necessary to provide appropriate services.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) These records must be maintained by the social worker for at least seven years after the last date of service to the client. Social workers who are employed by an agency or other entity are not required to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) maintain personal or separate records; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) personally retain records at the conclusion of their employment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.225.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Termination of services.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A social worker must terminate a professional relationship with a client when the social worker reasonably determines that the client is not likely to benefit from continued services or the services are no longer needed, unless the social worker is required by law to provide services. A social worker who anticipates terminating services must give reasonable notice to the client in a manner that is appropriate to the needs of the client. The social worker must provide appropriate referrals as needed or upon request of the client.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.225.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.108.0">2007 c 123 s 108,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.230">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.230 CONFIDENTIALITY AND RECORDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.230.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Informed consent.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A social worker must obtain valid, informed consent, appropriate to the circumstances, before providing services to clients. When obtaining informed consent, the social worker must determine whether the client has the capacity to provide informed consent. If the client does not have the capacity to provide consent, the social worker must obtain consent for the services from the client's legal representative. The social worker must not provide services, unless authorized or required by law, if the client or the client's legal representative does not consent to the services.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If a social worker determines that a client does not have the capacity to provide consent, and the client does not have a legal representative, the social worker:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) must, except as provided in clause (2), secure a legal representative for a client before providing services; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) may, notwithstanding clause (1), provide services, except when prohibited by other applicable law, that are necessary to ensure the client's safety or to preserve the client's property or financial resources.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A social worker must use clear and understandable language, including using an interpreter proficient in the client's primary language as necessary, to inform clients of the plan of services, risks related to the plan, limits to services, relevant costs, terms of payment, reasonable alternatives, the client's right to refuse or withdraw consent, and the time frame covered by the consent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.230.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Mandatory reporting and disclosure of client information.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">At the beginning of a professional relationship and during the professional relationship as necessary and appropriate, a social worker must inform the client of those circumstances under which the social worker may be required to disclose client information specified in subdivision 3, paragraph (a), without the client's consent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.230.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Confidentiality of client information.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A social worker must ensure the confidentiality of all client information obtained in the course of the social worker/client relationship and all client information otherwise obtained by the social worker that is relevant to the social worker/client relationship. Except as provided in this section, client information may be disclosed or released only with the client's or the client's legal representative's valid informed consent, appropriate to the circumstances, except when otherwise required by law. A social worker must seek consent to disclose or release client information only when such disclosure or release is necessary to provide social work services.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A social worker must continue to maintain confidentiality of the client information specified in paragraph (a) upon termination of the professional relationship including upon the death of the client, except as provided under this section or other applicable law.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A social worker must limit access to the client information specified in paragraph (a) in a social worker's agency to appropriate agency staff whose duties require access.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.230.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Release of client information with written informed consent.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as provided in subdivision 5, client information specified in subdivision 3, paragraph (a), may be released only with the client's or the client's legal representative's written informed consent. The written informed consent must:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) explain to whom the client's records may be released;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) explain the purpose for the release; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) state an expiration date for the authorized release of the records.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A social worker may provide client information specified in subdivision 3, paragraph (a), to a third party for the purpose of payment for services rendered only with the client's written informed consent.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Except as provided in subdivision 5, a social worker may disclose client information specified in subdivision 3, paragraph (a), only with the client's or the client's legal representative's written informed consent. When it is not practical to obtain written informed consent before providing necessary services, a social worker may disclose or release client information with the client's or the client's legal representative's oral informed consent.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Unless otherwise authorized by law, a social worker must obtain a client's written informed consent before taking a photograph of the client or making an audio or video recording of the client, or allowing a third party to do the same. The written informed consent must explain:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the purpose of the photograph or the recording and how the photograph or recording will be used, how it will be stored, and when it will be destroyed; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) how the client may have access to the photograph or recording.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.230.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Release of client information without written informed consent.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A social worker may disclose client information specified in subdivision 3, paragraph (a), without the written consent of the client or the client's legal representative only under the following circumstances or under the circumstances described in paragraph (b):</p>
          <p class="in">(1) when mandated or authorized by federal or state law, including the mandatory reporting requirements under the duty to warn, maltreatment of minors, and vulnerable adult laws specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.240">148E.240</a>, subdivisions 6 to 8;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) when the board issues a subpoena to the social worker; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) when a court of competent jurisdiction orders release of the client records or information.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) When providing services authorized or required by law to a client who does not have the capacity to provide consent and who does not have a legal representative, a social worker must disclose or release client records or information as necessary to provide services to ensure the client's safety or to preserve the client's property or financial resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.230.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Release of client records or information.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When releasing client records or information under this section, a social worker must release current, accurate, and complete records or information.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.230.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.109.0">2007 c 123 s 109,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.235">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.235 FEES AND BILLING PRACTICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.235.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Fees and payments.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A social worker must ensure that a client or a client's legal representative is informed of all fees at the initial session or meeting with the client, and that payment for services is arranged with the client or the client's legal representative at the beginning of the professional relationship. Upon request from a client or a client's legal representative, a social worker must provide in a timely manner a written payment plan or a written explanation of the charges for any services rendered.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) When providing services authorized or required by law to a client who does not have the capacity to provide consent and who does not have a legal representative, a social worker may submit reasonable bills to an appropriate payer for services provided.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.235.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Billing for services not provided.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A social worker must not bill for services that have not been provided except that, with prior notice to the client, a social worker may bill for failed appointments or for cancellations without sufficient notice. A social worker may bill only for provided services which are necessary and appropriate. Financial responsibility for failed appointment billings resides solely with the client and such costs may not be billed to public or private payers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.235.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">No payment for referrals.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A social worker must not accept or give a commission, rebate, or other form of remuneration solely or primarily to profit from the referral of a client.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.235.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Fees and billing practices.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A social worker must not engage in improper or fraudulent billing practices, including, but not limited to, violations of the federal Medicare and Medicaid laws or state medical assistance laws.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.235.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.110.0">2007 c 123 s 110,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.240">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.240 REPORTING REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.240.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Failure to self-report adverse actions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The board has grounds to take action under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.255">148E.255</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.270">148E.270</a> when a social worker fails to report to the board within 90 days:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) having been disciplined, sanctioned, or found to have violated a state, territorial, provincial, or foreign licensing agency's laws or rules;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) having been convicted of committing a felony, gross misdemeanor, or misdemeanor reasonably related to the practice of social work;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) having had a finding or verdict of guilt, whether or not the adjudication of guilt is withheld or not entered, of committing a felony, gross misdemeanor, or misdemeanor reasonably related to the practice of social work;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) having admitted to committing, or entering a no contest plea to committing, a felony, gross misdemeanor, or misdemeanor reasonably related to the practice of social work; or</p>
          <p class="in">(5) having been denied licensure by a state, territorial, provincial, or foreign licensing agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.240.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Failure to submit application information.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The board has grounds to take action under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.255">148E.255</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.270">148E.270</a> when an applicant or licensee fails to submit with an application the following information:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the dates and dispositions of any malpractice settlements or awards made relating to the social work services provided by the applicant or licensee; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the dates and dispositions of any civil litigations or arbitrations relating to the social work services provided by the applicant or licensee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.240.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reporting other licensed health professionals.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An applicant or licensee must report to the appropriate health-related licensing board conduct by a licensed health professional which would constitute grounds for disciplinary action under the statutes and rules enforced by that board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.240.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reporting unlicensed practice.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An applicant or licensee must report to the board conduct by an unlicensed person which constitutes the practice of social work, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.010">148E.010</a>, except when the unlicensed person is exempt from licensure according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.065">148E.065</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.240.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Failure to report other applicants or licensees; unlicensed practice.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The board has grounds to take action under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.255">148E.255</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.270">148E.270</a> when an applicant or licensee fails to report to the board conduct by:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) another licensee or applicant which the applicant or licensee has reason to believe may reasonably constitute grounds for disciplinary action under this section; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) an unlicensed person that constitutes the practice of social work when a license is required to practice social work.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.240.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Duty to warn.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A licensee must comply with the duty to warn established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148.975">148.975</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision, "licensee" includes interns and students.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.240.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reporting maltreatment of minors.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An applicant or licensee must comply with the reporting of maltreatment of minors established by chapter 260E.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.240.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Reporting maltreatment of vulnerable adults.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An applicant or licensee must comply with the reporting of maltreatment of vulnerable adults established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.240.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Subpoenas.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The board may issue subpoenas according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.245">148E.245</a> and chapter 214 for the production of any reports required by this section or any related documents.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.240.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.111.0">2007 c 123 s 111,138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/28/#laws.0.3.0">2019 c 28 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.8.33.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 8 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.245">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.245 INVESTIGATIVE POWERS AND PROCEDURES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Subpoenas.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The board may issue subpoenas and compel the attendance of witnesses and the production of all necessary papers, books, records, documents, and other evidentiary material as part of its investigation of an applicant or licensee under this section or chapter 214.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If any person fails or refuses to appear or testify regarding any matter about which the person may be lawfully questioned, or fails or refuses to produce any papers, books, records, documents, or other evidentiary materials in the matter to be heard, after having been required by order of the board or by a subpoena of the board to do so, the board may institute a proceeding in any district court to enforce the board's order or subpoena.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The board or a designated member of the board acting on behalf of the board may issue subpoenas or administer oaths to witnesses or take affirmations. Depositions may be taken within or out of the state in the manner provided by law for the taking of depositions in civil actions.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A subpoena or other process or paper may be served upon any person named therein, by mail or by any officer authorized to serve subpoenas or other process or paper in civil actions, with the same fees and mileage and in the same manner as prescribed by law for service of process issued out of the district court of this state.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Fees, mileage, and other costs must be paid as the board directs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Classification of data.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Any records obtained as part of an investigation must be treated as investigative data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41">13.41</a> and be classified as confidential data.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), client records must be treated as private data under chapter 13. Client records must be protected as private data in the records of the board and in administrative or judicial proceedings unless the client authorizes the board in writing to make public the identity of the client or a portion or all of the client's records.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Mental or physical examination; substance use disorder evaluation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) If the board (1) has probable cause to believe that an applicant or licensee has violated a statute or rule enforced by the board or an order issued by the board; and (2) believes the applicant may have a health-related condition relevant to the violation, the board may issue an order directing the applicant or licensee to submit to one or more of the following: a mental examination, a physical examination, or a substance use disorder evaluation.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) An examination or evaluation order issued by the board must include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) factual specifications on which the order is based;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the purpose of the examination or evaluation;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the name of the person or entity that will conduct the examination or evaluation; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the means by which the examination or evaluation will be paid for.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Every applicant or licensee must submit to a mental examination, a physical examination, or a substance use disorder evaluation when ordered to do so in writing by the board.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) By submitting to a mental examination, a physical examination, or a substance use disorder evaluation, an applicant or licensee waives all objections to the admissibility of the examiner or evaluator's testimony or reports on the grounds that the testimony or reports constitute a privileged communication.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Failure to submit to an examination.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) If an applicant or licensee fails to submit to an examination or evaluation ordered by the board according to subdivision 3, unless the failure was due to circumstances beyond the control of the applicant or licensee, the failure is an admission that the applicant or licensee violated a statute or rule enforced by the board as specified in the examination or evaluation order issued by the board. The failure may result in an application being denied or other adversarial, corrective, or disciplinary action being taken by the board without a contested case hearing.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If an applicant or licensee requests a contested case hearing after the board denies an application or takes other disciplinary or adversarial action, the only issues which may be determined at the hearing are whether:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the board had probable cause to issue the examination or evaluation order; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the failure to submit to the examination or evaluation was due to circumstances beyond the control of the applicant or licensee.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Neither the record of a proceeding under this subdivision nor an order issued by the board may be admissible, subject to subpoena, or be used against the applicant or licensee in a proceeding in which the board is not a party or decision maker.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Information obtained under this subdivision must be treated as private data under chapter 13. An order issued by the board as the result of an applicant's or licensee's failure to submit to an examination or evaluation must be treated as public data under chapter 13.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Access to data and records.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) In addition to ordering a physical or mental examination or substance use disorder evaluation, and notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.384">13.384</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.651">144.651</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/595.02">595.02</a>, or any other statute limiting access to health records, the board or a designated member of the board acting on behalf of the board may subpoena physical, mental, and substance use disorder health records relating to an applicant or licensee without the applicant's or licensee's consent if the board has:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) probable cause to believe that the applicant or licensee has violated chapter 214, a statute or rule enforced by the board, or an order issued by the board; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) reason to believe that the records are relevant and necessary to the investigation.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) An applicant, licensee, insurance company, government agency, health care facility, or provider as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.291#stat.144.291.2">144.291, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (i), must comply with any subpoena of the board under this subdivision and is not liable in any action for damages for releasing information subpoenaed by the board under this subdivision unless the information provided is false and the person or entity providing the information knew or had reason to know that the information was false.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Information on individuals obtained under this subdivision must be treated as investigative data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41">13.41</a> and be classified as confidential data.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) If an applicant, licensee, person, or entity does not comply with any subpoena of the board under this subdivision, the board may institute a proceeding in any district court to enforce the board's subpoena.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Evidence of past sexual conduct.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">If, in a proceeding for taking action against an applicant or licensee under this section, the charges involve sexual contact with a client or former client, the board or administrative law judge must not consider evidence of the client's or former client's previous sexual conduct. Reference to the client's or former client's previous sexual conduct must not be made during the proceedings or in the findings, except by motion of the complainant, unless the evidence would be admissible under the applicable provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.347#stat.609.347.3">609.347, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Investigations involving vulnerable adults or children in need of protection.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), if the board receives a complaint about a social worker regarding the social worker's involvement in a case of vulnerable adults or children in need of protection, the county or other appropriate public authority may request that the board suspend its investigation, and the board must comply until such time as the court issues its findings on the case.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), the board may continue with an investigation if the board determines that doing so is in the best interests of the vulnerable adult or a child in need of protection and is consistent with the board's obligation to protect the public. If the board chooses to continue an investigation, the board must notify the county or other appropriate public authority in writing and state its reasons for doing so.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Notification of complainant.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) In no more than 14 calendar days after receiving a complaint regarding a licensee, the board must notify the complainant that the board has received the complaint.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The board must periodically notify the complainant of the status of the complaint.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Notification of licensee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), in no more than 60 calendar days after receiving a complaint regarding a licensee, the board must notify the licensee that the board has received the complaint and inform the licensee of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the substance of the complaint;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the sections of the law that allegedly have been violated; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) whether an investigation is being conducted.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply if the board determines that such notice:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) would compromise the board's investigation according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/214.10">214.10</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) cannot reasonably be accomplished within this time.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The board must periodically notify the licensee of the status of the complaint.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.245.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Resolution of complaints.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">In no more than one year after receiving a complaint regarding a licensee, the board must resolve or dismiss the complaint unless the board determines that resolving or dismissing the complaint cannot reasonably be accomplished within this time.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.245.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.112.0">2007 c 123 s 112,138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.10.15.0">2007 c 147 art 10 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.2.12.0">2020 c 83 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.250">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.250 OBLIGATION TO COOPERATE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.250.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Obligation to cooperate.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An applicant or licensee who is the subject of an investigation, or who is questioned by or on behalf of the board in connection with an investigation, must cooperate fully with the investigation. Cooperation includes, but is not limited to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) responding fully and promptly to any question relating to the investigation;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) as reasonably requested by the board, providing copies of client and other records in the applicant's or licensee's possession relating to the investigation;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) executing release of records as reasonably requested by the board; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) appearing at conferences, hearings, or meetings scheduled by the board, as required in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.255">148E.255</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.270">148E.270</a> and chapter 214.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.250.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Investigation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A social worker must not knowingly withhold relevant information, give false or misleading information, or do anything to obstruct an investigation of the social worker or another social worker by the board or by another state or federal regulatory or law enforcement authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.250.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Payment for copies.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The board must pay for copies requested by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.250.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Access to client records.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, an applicant or licensee must allow the board access to any records of a client provided services by the applicant or licensee under investigation. If the client has not signed a consent permitting access to the client's records, the applicant or licensee must delete any data in the records that identifies the client before providing the records to the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.250.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Classification of data.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Any records obtained according to this subdivision must be treated as investigative data according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41">13.41</a> and be classified as confidential data.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.250.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.113.0">2007 c 123 s 113,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.255">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.255 TYPES OF ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.255.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Actions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The board may take disciplinary action according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.260">148E.260</a>, adversarial but nondisciplinary action according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.265">148E.265</a>, or voluntary action according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.270">148E.270</a>. Any action taken under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.260">148E.260</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.270">148E.270</a> is public data.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.255.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disciplinary action.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.260">148E.260</a>, "disciplinary action" means an action taken by the board against an applicant or licensee that addresses a complaint alleging a violation of a statute or rule the board is empowered to enforce.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.255.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Adversarial but nondisciplinary action.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.265">148E.265</a>, "adversarial but nondisciplinary action" means a nondisciplinary action taken by the board that addresses a complaint alleging a violation of a statute or rule the board is empowered to enforce.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.255.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Voluntary action.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.270">148E.270</a>, "voluntary action" means a nondisciplinary action agreed to by the board or a designated board member and an applicant or licensee that, through educational or other corrective means, addresses a complaint alleging a violation of a statute or rule that the board is empowered to enforce.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.255.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.114.0">2007 c 123 s 114,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.260">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.260 DISCIPLINARY ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.260.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General disciplinary actions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) When the board has grounds for disciplinary actions under this chapter, the board may take one or more of the following disciplinary actions:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) deny an application;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) permanently revoke a license to practice social work;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) indefinitely or temporarily suspend a license to practice social work;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) impose restrictions on a licensee's scope of practice;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) impose conditions required for the licensee to maintain licensure, including, but not limited to, additional education, supervision, and requiring the passing of an examination provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.055">148E.055</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) reprimand a licensee;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) impose a civil penalty of up to $10,000 for each violation in order to discourage future violations or to deprive the licensee of any economic advantage gained by reason of the violation; or</p>
          <p class="in">(8) impose a fee to reimburse the board for all or part of the cost of the proceedings resulting in disciplinary action, including, but not limited to, the amount paid by the board for services received from or expenses incurred by the Office of Administrative Hearings, the Office of the Attorney General, court reporters, witnesses, board members, board staff, or the amount paid by the board for reproducing records.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Disciplinary action taken by the board under this subdivision is in effect pending determination of an appeal unless the court, upon petition and for good cause shown, decides otherwise.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.260.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reprimands.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) In addition to the board's authority to issue a reprimand according to subdivision 1, a designated board member reviewing a complaint as provided for in chapter 214 may issue a reprimand to a licensee. The designated board member must notify the licensee that the reprimand will become final disciplinary action unless the licensee requests a hearing by the board within 14 calendar days.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If the licensee requests a hearing within 14 calendar days, the board must schedule a hearing unless the designated board member withdraws the reprimand.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The hearing must be scheduled within 14 working days of the time the licensee submits a request for the hearing.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The designated board member who issued the reprimand may participate in the hearing but must not deliberate or vote on the decision by the board.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The only evidence permitted at the hearing is affidavits or other documents except for testimony by the licensee or other witnesses whose testimony the board chair has authorized for good cause.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) If testimony is authorized, the testimony is subject to cross-examination.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) After the hearing, the board must affirm or dismiss the reprimand.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.260.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Temporary suspensions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) In addition to any other remedy provided by statute, the board or a designated board member may, without a hearing, temporarily suspend a license to practice social work if the board or the designated board member finds that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the licensee has violated a statute or rule enforced by the board, any other federal or state law or rule related to the practice of social work, or an order, stipulation, or agreement agreed to or issued by the board; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) continued practice by the licensee would create a serious risk of harm to others.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The suspension is in effect upon service of a written order on the licensee specifying the statute, rule, order, stipulation, or agreement violated. Service of the order is effective if the order is served on the licensee or the licensee's attorney personally or by first class mail to the most recent address provided to the board for the licensee or the licensee's attorney.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The temporary suspension remains in effect until after the board issues an order according to paragraph (e), or if there is a contested case hearing, after the board issues a written final order according to paragraph (g).</p>
          <p class="in">(d) If the licensee requests in writing within five calendar days of service of the order that the board hold a hearing, the board must hold a hearing on the sole issue of whether to continue, modify, or lift the suspension. The board must hold the hearing within ten working days of receipt of the licensee's written request. Evidence presented by the board or licensee must be in affidavit form only, except that the licensee or the licensee's attorney may present oral argument.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Within five working days after the hearing, the board must issue its order. If the licensee contests the order, the board must schedule a contested case hearing under chapter 14. The contested case hearing must be scheduled to occur within 45 calendar days after issuance of the order.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The administrative law judge must issue a report within 30 calendar days after the contested case hearing is concluded.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) The board must issue a final order within 30 calendar days after the board receives the administrative law judge's report.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.260.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.115.0">2007 c 123 s 115,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.265">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.265 ADVERSARIAL BUT NONDISCIPLINARY ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.265.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Automatic suspensions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A license to practice social work is automatically suspended if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a guardian of a licensee is appointed by order of a court according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-101">524.5-101</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-102">524.5-102</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the licensee is committed by order of a court according to chapter 253B.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A license remains suspended until:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the licensee is restored to capacity by a court; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) upon petition by the licensee and after a hearing or an agreement with the licensee, the board terminates the suspension.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) If the board terminates the suspension, it may do so with or without conditions or restrictions, including, but not limited to, participation in the health professional services program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.265.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cease and desist orders.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The board or a designated board member may issue a cease and desist order to stop a person from engaging in unauthorized practice or from violating or threatening to violate a statute or rule enforced by the board or an order, stipulation, or agreement agreed to or issued by the board.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The cease and desist order must state the reason for its issuance and give notice of the person's right to request a hearing under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.62">14.62</a>. If the person fails to request a hearing in writing postmarked within 15 calendar days after service of the cease and desist order, the order is the final order of the board and is not reviewable by a court or agency.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) If the board receives a written request for a hearing postmarked within 15 calendar days after service of the cease and desist order, the board must schedule a hearing within 30 calendar days of receiving the request.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The administrative law judge must issue a report within 30 calendar days after the contested case hearing is concluded.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Within 30 calendar days after the board receives the administrative law judge's report, the board must issue a final order modifying, vacating, or making permanent the cease and desist order. The final order remains in effect until modified or vacated by the board.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) If a person does not comply with a cease and desist order, the board may institute a proceeding in any district court to obtain injunctive relief or other appropriate relief, including but not limited to, a civil penalty payable to the board of up to $10,000 for each violation.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) A cease and desist order issued according to this subdivision does not relieve a person from criminal prosecution by a competent authority or from disciplinary action by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.265.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Injunctive relief.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) In addition to any other remedy provided by law, the board may bring an action in district court for injunctive relief to restrain any unauthorized practice or violation or threatened violation of any statute or rule, stipulation, or agreement agreed to or enforced by the board or an order issued by the board.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A temporary restraining order may be granted in the proceeding if continued activity by a person would create an imminent risk of harm to others.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Injunctive relief granted according to this subdivision does not relieve a person from criminal prosecution by a competent authority or from disciplinary action by the board.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) In bringing an action for injunctive relief, the board need not show irreparable harm.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.265.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.116.0">2007 c 123 s 116,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.270">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.270 VOLUNTARY ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.270.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Agreements for corrective action.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The board or a designated board member may enter into an agreement for corrective action with an applicant or licensee when the board or a designated board member determines that a complaint alleging a violation of a statute or rule enforced by the board or an order issued by the board may best be resolved through an agreement for corrective action when disciplinary action is not required to protect the public.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) An agreement for corrective action must:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) be in writing;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) specify the facts upon which the agreement is based;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) clearly indicate the corrective action agreed upon; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) provide that the complaint that resulted in the agreement must be dismissed by the board or the designated board member upon successful completion of the corrective action.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The board or designated board member may determine successful completion when the applicant or licensee submits a request for dismissal that documents the applicant's or licensee's successful completion of the corrective action. The burden of proof is on the applicant or licensee to prove successful completion.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) An agreement for corrective action is not disciplinary action but must be treated as public data under chapter 13.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The board may impose a fee to reimburse the board for all or part of the costs of the proceedings resulting in a corrective action, including, but not limited to, the amount paid by the board for services received from or expenses incurred by the Office of the Attorney General, board members, board staff, or the amount paid by the board for reproducing records.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The board or designated board member must not enter into an agreement for corrective action when the complaint alleged sexual conduct with a client unless there is insufficient evidence to justify disciplinary action but there is a basis for corrective action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.270.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Stipulations to cease practicing social work.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The board or a designated board member may enter into a stipulation to cease practicing social work with a licensee if the board or designated board member determines that the licensee is unable to practice social work competently or safely or that the social worker's continued practice creates an unacceptable risk of safety to clients, potential clients, or the public.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A stipulation to cease practicing social work must:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) be in writing;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) specify the facts upon which the stipulation is based;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) clearly indicate that the licensee must not practice social work and must not hold out to the public that the social worker is licensed; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) specify the term of the stipulation or when and under what circumstances the licensee may petition the board for termination of the stipulation.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A stipulation to cease practicing social work is not disciplinary action but must be treated as public data under chapter 13.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Nothing in this subdivision prevents the board or designated board member from taking any other disciplinary or adversarial action authorized by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.255">148E.255</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.265">148E.265</a> in lieu of or in addition to entering into a stipulation to cease practicing social work.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.270.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.117.0">2007 c 123 s 117,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.275">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.275 UNAUTHORIZED PRACTICE.</h1>
        <p class="in">No individual may:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) engage in the practice of social work without a social work license under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.055">148E.055</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.060">148E.060</a>, except when the individual is exempt from licensure according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.065">148E.065</a>;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) provide social work services to a client who resides in this state when the individual providing the services is not licensed as a social worker according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.055">148E.055</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.060">148E.060</a>, except when the individual is exempt from licensure according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.065">148E.065</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.275.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.118.0">2007 c 123 s 118,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.280">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.280 USE OF TITLES.</h1>
        <p> No individual may be presented to the public by any title incorporating the words "social work" or "social worker" or in the titles in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.195">148E.195</a>, unless that individual is employed by a county or holds a license under this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.280.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.119.0">2007 c 123 s 119,138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/197/#laws.1.18.0">2012 c 197 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.37.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 37</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.285">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.285 REPORTING REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.285.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Institutions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A state agency, political subdivision, agency of a local unit of government, private agency, hospital, clinic, prepaid medical plan, or other health care institution or organization must report to the board:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) any adversarial action, disciplinary action, or other sanction for conduct that might constitute grounds for action under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.190">148E.190</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the resignation of any applicant or licensee prior to the conclusion of any proceeding for adversarial action, disciplinary action, or other sanction for conduct that might constitute grounds for action under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.190">148E.190</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the resignation of any applicant or licensee prior to the commencement of a proceeding for adversarial action, disciplinary action, or other sanction for conduct that might constitute grounds for action under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.190">148E.190</a>, but after the applicant or licensee had knowledge that a proceeding was contemplated or in preparation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.285.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Professional societies and associations.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A state or local professional society or association whose members consist primarily of licensed social workers must report to the board any adversarial action, disciplinary action, or other sanction taken against a member.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.285.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Immunity.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An individual, professional society or association, state agency, political subdivision, agency of a local unit of government, private agency, hospital, clinic, prepaid medical plan, other health care institution or organization, or other entity is immune from civil liability or criminal prosecution for submitting in good faith a report under subdivision 1 or 2 or for otherwise reporting, providing information, or testifying about violations or alleged violations of this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.148E.285.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Failure to report.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">On or after August 1, 2012, any person, institution, or organization that fails to report as required under subdivisions 1 and 2 shall be subject to civil penalties for failing to report as required by law.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.285.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.120.0">2007 c 123 s 120,138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/278/#laws.2.17.0">2012 c 278 art 2 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.290">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.290 PENALTIES.</h1>
        <p class="in">An individual or other entity that violates section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.275">148E.275</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.280">148E.280</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.285">148E.285</a> is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.290.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/123/#laws.0.121.0">2007 c 123 s 121,138</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.40">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.40 TITLE.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.40">148E.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.55">148E.55</a> shall be known and cited as the social work services licensure compact.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.32.1.0">2024 c 127 art 32 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.41">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.41 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <p>As used in this Compact, and except as otherwise provided, the following definitions shall apply:</p>
        <p>(1) "Active military member" means any individual with full-time duty status in the active armed forces of the United States, including members of the National Guard and Reserve.</p>
        <p>(2) "Adverse action" means any administrative, civil, equitable, or criminal action permitted by a state's laws which is imposed by a licensing authority or other authority against a regulated social worker, including actions against an individual's license or multistate authorization to practice such as revocation, suspension, probation, monitoring of the licensee, limitation on the licensee's practice, or any other encumbrance on licensure affecting a regulated social worker's authorization to practice, including issuance of a cease and desist action.</p>
        <p>(3) "Alternative program" means a nondisciplinary monitoring or practice remediation process approved by a licensing authority to address practitioners with an impairment.</p>
        <p>(4) "Charter member states" means member states who have enacted legislation to adopt this Compact where such legislation predates the effective date of this Compact as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.53">148E.53</a>.</p>
        <p>(5) "Compact" means sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.40">148E.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.55">148E.55</a>.</p>
        <p>(6) "Compact Commission" or "Commission" means the government agency whose membership consists of all States that have enacted this Compact, which is known as the Social Work Licensure Compact Commission, as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.49">148E.49</a>, and which shall operate as an instrumentality of the member states.</p>
        <p>(7) "Current significant investigative information" means:</p>
        <p>(i) investigative information that a licensing authority, after a preliminary inquiry that includes notification and an opportunity for the regulated social worker to respond, has reason to believe is not groundless and, if proved true, would indicate more than a minor infraction as may be defined by the Commission; or</p>
        <p>(ii) investigative information that indicates that the regulated social worker represents an immediate threat to public health and safety, as may be defined by the Commission, regardless of whether the regulated social worker has been notified and has had an opportunity to respond.</p>
        <p>(8) "Data system" means a repository of information about licensees, including continuing education, examinations, licensure, current significant investigative information, disqualifying events, multistate licenses, and adverse action information or other information as required by the Commission.</p>
        <p>(9) "Disqualifying event" means any adverse action or incident which results in an encumbrance that disqualifies or makes the licensee ineligible to obtain, retain, or renew a multistate license.</p>
        <p>(10) "Domicile" means the jurisdiction in which the licensee resides and intends to remain indefinitely.</p>
        <p>(11) "Encumbrance" means a revocation or suspension of, or any limitation on, the full and unrestricted practice of social work licensed and regulated by a licensing authority.</p>
        <p>(12) "Executive Committee" means a group of delegates elected or appointed to act on behalf of, and within the powers granted to them by, the Compact and Commission.</p>
        <p>(13) "Home state" means the member state that is the licensee's primary domicile.</p>
        <p>(14) "Impairment" means a condition that may impair a practitioner's ability to engage in full and unrestricted practice as a regulated social worker without some type of intervention and may include alcohol and drug dependence, mental health impairment, and neurological or physical impairments.</p>
        <p>(15) "Licensee" means an individual who currently holds a license from a state to practice as a regulated social worker.</p>
        <p>(16) "Licensing authority" means the board or agency of a member state, or equivalent, that is responsible for the licensing and regulation of regulated social workers.</p>
        <p>(17) "Member state" means a state, commonwealth, district, or territory of the United States of America that has enacted this Compact.</p>
        <p>(18) "Multistate authorization to practice" means a legally authorized privilege to practice, which is equivalent to a license, associated with a multistate license permitting the practice of social work in a remote state.</p>
        <p>(19) "Multistate license" means a license to practice as a regulated social worker issued by a home state licensing authority that authorizes the regulated social worker to practice in all member states under multistate authorization to practice.</p>
        <p>(20) "Qualifying national exam" means a national licensing examination approved by the Commission.</p>
        <p>(21) "Regulated social worker" means any clinical, master's, or bachelor's social worker licensed by a member state regardless of the title used by that member state.</p>
        <p>(22) "Remote state" means a member state other than the licensee's home state.</p>
        <p>(23) "Rule" or "rule of the Commission" means a regulation or regulations duly promulgated by the Commission, as authorized by the Compact, that has the force of law.</p>
        <p>(24) "Single state license" means a social work license issued by any state that authorizes practice only within the issuing state and does not include multistate authorization to practice in any member state.</p>
        <p>(25) "Social work" or "social work services" means the application of social work theory, knowledge, methods, ethics, and the professional use of self to restore or enhance social, psychosocial, or biopsychosocial functioning of individuals, couples, families, groups, organizations, and communities through the care and services provided by a regulated social worker as set forth in the member state's statutes and regulations in the state where the services are being provided.</p>
        <p>(26) "State" means any state, commonwealth, district, or territory of the United States of America that regulates the practice of social work.</p>
        <p>(27) "Unencumbered license" means a license that authorizes a regulated social worker to engage in the full and unrestricted practice of social work.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.32.2.0">2024 c 127 art 32 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.42">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.42 STATE PARTICIPATION IN THE COMPACT.</h1>
        <p>(a) To be eligible to participate in the compact, a potential member state must currently meet all of the following criteria:</p>
        <p>(1) license and regulate the practice of social work at either the clinical, master's, or bachelor's category;</p>
        <p>(2) require applicants for licensure to graduate from a program that:</p>
        <p>(i) is operated by a college or university recognized by the licensing authority;</p>
        <p>(ii) is accredited, or in candidacy by an institution that subsequently becomes accredited, by an accrediting agency recognized by either:</p>
        <p>(A) the Council for Higher Education Accreditation, or its successor; or</p>
        <p>(B) the United States Department of Education; and</p>
        <p>(iii) corresponds to the licensure sought as outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.43">148E.43</a>;</p>
        <p>(3) require applicants for clinical licensure to complete a period of supervised practice; and</p>
        <p>(4) have a mechanism in place for receiving, investigating, and adjudicating complaints about licensees.</p>
        <p>(b) To maintain membership in the Compact, a member state shall:</p>
        <p>(1) require that applicants for a multistate license pass a qualifying national exam for the corresponding category of multistate license sought as outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.43">148E.43</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) participate fully in the Commission's data system, including using the Commission's unique identifier as defined in rules;</p>
        <p>(3) notify the Commission, in compliance with the terms of the Compact and rules, of any adverse action or the availability of current significant investigative information regarding a licensee;</p>
        <p>(4) implement procedures for considering the criminal history records of applicants for a multistate license. Such procedures shall include the submission of fingerprints or other biometric-based information by applicants for the purpose of obtaining an applicant's criminal history record information from the Federal Bureau of Investigation and the agency responsible for retaining that state's criminal records;</p>
        <p>(5) comply with the rules of the Commission;</p>
        <p>(6) require an applicant to obtain or retain a license in the home state and meet the home state's qualifications for licensure or renewal of licensure, as well as all other applicable home state laws;</p>
        <p>(7) authorize a licensee holding a multistate license in any member state to practice in accordance with the terms of the Compact and rules of the Commission; and</p>
        <p>(8) designate a delegate to participate in the Commission meetings.</p>
        <p>(c) A member state meeting the requirements of paragraphs (a) and (b) shall designate the categories of social work licensure that are eligible for issuance of a multistate license for applicants in such member state. To the extent that any member state does not meet the requirements for participation in the Compact at any particular category of social work licensure, such member state may choose but is not obligated to issue a multistate license to applicants that otherwise meet the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148E.43">148E.43</a> for issuance of a multistate license in such category or categories of licensure.</p>
        <p>(d) The home state may charge a fee for granting the multistate license.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.148E.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.32.3.0">2024 c 127 art 32 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.148E.43">
        <h1 class="shn">148E.43 SOCIAL WORKER PARTICIPATION IN THE COMPACT.</h1>
        <p>(a) To be eligible for a multistate license under the terms and provisions of the Compact, an applicant, regardless of category, must:</p>
        <p>(1) hold or be eligible for an active, unencumbered license in the home state;</p>
        <p>(2) pay any applicable fees, including any state fee, for the multistate license;</p>
        <p>(3) submit, in connection with an application for a multistate license, fingerprints or other biometric data for the purpose of obtaining criminal history record information from the Federal Bureau of Investigation and the agency responsible for retaining that state's criminal records;</p>
        <p>(4) notify the home state of any adverse action, encumbrance, or restriction on any professional license taken by any member state or nonmember state within 30 days from the date the action is taken;</p>
        <p>(5) meet any continuing competence requirements established by the home state; and</p>
        <p>(6) abide by the laws, regulations, and applicable standards in the member state where the client is located at the time care is rendered.</p>
        <p>(b) An applicant for a clinical-category multistate license must meet all of the following requirements:</p>
        <p>(1) fulfill a competency requirement, which shall be satisfied by either:</p>
        <p>(i) passage of a clinical-category qualifying national exam;</p>
        <p>(ii) licensure of the applicant in their home state at the clinical category, beginning prior to such time as a qualifying national exam was required by the home state and accompanied by a period of continuous social work licensure thereafter, all of which may be further governed by the rules of the Commission; or</p>
        <p>(iii) the substantial equivalency of the foregoing competency requirements which the Commission may determine by rule;</p>
        <p>(2) attain at least a master's degree in social work from a program that is:</p>
        <p>(i) operated by a college or university recognized by the licensing authority; and</p>
        <p>(ii) accredited, or in candidacy that subsequently becomes accredited, by an accrediting agency recognized by either:</p>
        <p>(A) the Council for Higher Education Accreditation or its successor; or</p>
        <p>(B) the United States Department of Education; and</p>
        <p>(3) fulfill a practice requirement, which shall be satisfied by demonstrating completion of:</p>
        <p>(i) a period of postgraduate supervised clinical practice equal to a minimum of 3,000 hours;</p>
        <p>(ii) a minimum of two years of full-time postgraduate supervised clinical practice; or</p>
        <p>(iii) the substantial equivalency of the foregoing practice requirements which the Commission may determine by rule.</p>
